th entry and zero elsewhere.
So by Lemma
3
(iv),
7
(iv),
10
(i), and using (
A81
) , we get

\left\|\bm{IV}_{b}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{nT^{3/2}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right).

(A82)

Similarly, because of Lemma
2
(i),
2
(iii),
3
(iii),
7
(iv), and
10
(i),

\left\|\bm{IV}_{c}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right).

(A83)

Moreover, by Proposition
1
(a), Lemma
3
(i),
3
(ii), , and Lemma
7
(iv) and
10
(i)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{IV}_{d}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A84)

Similarly, by Proposition
1
(a), Lemma
2
(i),
7
(iv), and
10
(i), and using (
A81
),

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{IV}_{e}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{nT^{3/2}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{3/2}\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\right)\right).

(A85)

And, finally, by Proposition
1
(a), Lemma
2
(iii) , and Lemma
3
(iii),
7
(iv), and
10
(i),

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{IV}_{f}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A86)

By using (
A80
), (
A82
), (
A83
), (
A84
), (
A85
), and (
A86
)
in (
A79
) we get

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})}{n\sqrt{T}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A87)

The proof of part (b) follows from Lemma
2
(ii),
3
(ii),
11
(i), and
7
(iv), and using (
A78
) and (
A87
) in (
A77
).

For part (c), because of part (b), and Lemma
2
(ii) and
11
(ii),
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{F}}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{F}}}{T}-\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime}}{T}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{(\widehat{\bm{F}}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\bm{F}^{\prime}+\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\bm{F}^{\prime})(\widehat{\bm{F}}-\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime}+\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime})}{T}-\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime}}{T}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{F}}-\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{F}}-\bm{F}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1})^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{T},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right).

This proves part (c) and completes the proof.

\Box

A.8
Proof of Proposition
7

For part (a), Assumption
9
(b) implies
\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}
. Therefore, since the non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}
are the same as the
r
eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
, which is diagonal by Assumption
9
(a). Then, we must have, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}.

This proves part (a).

For part (b), since
\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}
, it must be that

\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2},

(A88)

for some
r\times r
invertible
\mathbf{K}_{*}
. Notice that since
\mathbf{K}_{*}
is a special case of the matrix
\mathbf{K}
defined in (
A3
) in the proof of Proposition
1
(a), then
\mathbf{K}_{*}
is finite and positive definite because of Lemma
9
.

Now, since
\text{rk}(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}})=r
for all
n>N
(see the proof of Proposition
1
(a)):

\mathbf{K}_{*}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2},

(A89)

which is also obtained by linear projection, and

\mathbf{K}^{-1}_{*}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}{\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}.

(A90)

Moreover, from (
A89
), because fo Assumption
9
(b) and part (a):

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{*}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}_{r}.

(A91)

So because of (
A91
), we have that
\mathbf{K}_{*}
is an orthogonal matrix, i.e.,
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}
.
Finally, by (
A89
) we also have

\mathbf{V}^{\chi}=\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}

(A92)

and by substituting (
A92
) into (
A90
), because of (
A91
),

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda},

which is equivalent to:

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{M}^{\chi},

(A93)

and by part (a), we must have
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Alternatively, by multiplying on the right both sides of (
A88
) by their transposed:

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{M}^{\chi},

(A94)

since eigenvectors are normalized, and again by part (a), we must have
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. So from (
A88
) or (
A94
) we prove part (b).

For part (c), since
\bm{\chi}_{t}=\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{F}_{t}
, then by linear projection of
\bm{\Lambda}
onto
\bm{\chi}_{t}
, and using parts (a) and (b), for all
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\mathbf{F}_{t}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\chi}_{t}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\chi}_{t}.

This proves part (c).

For part (d), from Assumption
1
(a) and part (a), as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\right\|=\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\right\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

and from Lemma
8
(i), as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\right\|=o(1).

By uniqueness of the limit we prove part (d).

Part (e) follows directly from Assumptions
1
(c) and
9
(b). This completes the proof.
\Box

A.9
Proof of Proposition
8

Because of (
A56
) in the proof of Proposition
5
(a), using (
13
)

\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}+\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}})}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A95)

Or, equivalently,

\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A96)

Moreover, by Assumption
9
and using (
A96
) in (
28
) we have

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A97)

Therefore, because of (
A97
), as
n,T\to\infty
,
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is an
r\times r
orthogonal matrix thus it has eigenvalues
\pm 1
.

Moreover, because of (
A57
) in the proof of Proposition
5
(a)

\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A98)

Thus, from (
A97
) and (
A98
) and by Lemma
7
(iv),

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A99)

And from (
A99
) it follows that

\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(A100)

So, because of (
A100
), as
n,T\to\infty
, the columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
are the eigenvectors of
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
with eigenvalues
\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}
. The eigenvectors are normalized since
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is orthogonal, as
n,T\to\infty
. Moreover, under Assumption
9
,
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
is diagonal, so, as
n,T\to\infty
,
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
must be diagonal with eigenvalues
\pm 1
. By noticing that, under Assumption
5
(c),
\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)=o_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right)
, we prove part (a).

Part (b) follows from the fact that
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is orthogonal, as
n,T\to\infty
. This completes the proof.
\Box

Appendix B
Auxiliary results

B.1
Major auxiliary lemmata

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
:

(i)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{js}]|\leq M_{1\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{1\xi}
independent of
n
and
T
;

(ii)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|\leq M_{2\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{1\xi}
independent of
n
and
t
;

(iii)

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\!\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]|\leq M_{3\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{3\xi}
independent of
i
and
T
;

(iv)

for all
j=1,\ldots,r
,
\underline{C}_{j}\!\leq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\!\overline{C}_{j}
,
for some finite positive reals
\underline{C}_{j}
and
\overline{C}_{j}
;

(v)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mu_{1}^{\xi}\leq M_{\xi}
, where
M_{\xi}
is defined in Assumption
2
(b);

(vi)

for all
j=1,\ldots,r
,
\underline{C}_{j}\leq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{x}}{n}\leq\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{x}}{n}\leq\overline{C}_{j}
, and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mu_{r+1}^{x}\leq M_{\xi}
, where
M_{\xi}
is defined in Assumption
2
(b).

Proof.
Using Assumption
2
(b), we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{js}]|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=-(T-1)}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{|k|}{T}\right)|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{j,t-k}]|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho^{|k|}M_{ij}\leq\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{ij}\leq M_{\xi},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=-(T-1)}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{|k|}{T}\right)|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{i,t-k}]|\leq\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho^{|k|}M_{ii}\leq\frac{1+\rho}{1-\rho}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{ii}\leq\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}.

Defining,
M_{1\xi}=M_{3\xi}=\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}
and
M_{2\xi}=M_{\xi}
, we prove parts (i), (ii), and (iii).

For part (iv), by
Merikoski and
Kumar 2004
, for all
j=1,\ldots,r
, we have

\frac{\mu_{r}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}\mu_{j}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\frac{\mu_{j}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}\ \mu_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})

(B1)

and the proof follows from Assumption
1
(a) which, by continuity of eigenvalues, implies that, for any
j=1,\ldots,r
, as
n\to\infty

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}=\mu_{j}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda})

with (see also (
2
) and Lemma
3
(iv))

0<m_{\Lambda}^{2}\leq\mu_{r}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda})\leq\mu_{1}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda})\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}<\infty,

and by Assumption
1
(b) and Assumption
1
(c) which imply (see also (
3
) and Lemma
3
(v))

0<m_{F}\leq\mu_{r}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq\mu_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq M_{F}<\infty.

For part (v), by Assumption
2
(b):

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{ij}\leq M_{\xi}.

Part (vi) follows from parts (iv) and (v) and Weyl’s inequality. This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 2
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, for all
t=1,\ldots,T
and all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
and
\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iii)

\|\frac{\bm{\xi}_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
and
\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{nT}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iv)

\|\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
and
\|\frac{\bm{X}}{\sqrt{nT}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.
By Assumption
1
(a), which holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}_{F}=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{ij}^{2}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2},

since
M_{\Lambda}
is independent of
i
. This proves part (i).

By Assumption
1
(b), which holds for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
because of stationarity (see also part (i) of Lemma
5
),

\displaystyle\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]\leq r\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq r\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\bm{\eta}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\eta}_{j}\leq r\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\|\leq rM_{F},

(B2)

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and where
\bm{\eta}_{j}
is an
r
-dimensional vector with one in the
j
th entry and zero elsewhere. Therefore, from (
B.1
):

\displaystyle\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[[\bm{F}]_{tj}^{2}]\leq\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|^{2}]\leq rM_{F}.

This proves part (ii).

Then, because of Lemma
1
(v), which holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and holds also for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
by stationarity, which is implied by Assumption
2
(b),

\displaystyle\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\xi}_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}^{2}]\leq\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\|=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{1}^{\xi}\leq M_{\xi}.

(B3)

since
M_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
t
and where
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
is an
n
-dimensional vector with one in the
i
th entry and zero elsewhere. Therefore, from (
B.1
)

\displaystyle\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[[\bm{\Xi}]_{ti}^{2}]\leq\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}^{2}]\leq M_{\xi}.

This proves part (iii).

Finally, as a consequence of parts (ii) and (iii) and Assumptions
1
(a) and
4
:

\displaystyle\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[x_{it}^{2}]\leq\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[x_{it}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\left\{\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\lambda}_{i}+\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\,\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\|+\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{F}+M_{\xi}=M_{X},\;\text{say,}

(B4)

where
M_{X}
is independent of
i
,
t
,
n
, and
T
. Therefore, from part (i) and (
B.1
):

\displaystyle\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{X}}{\sqrt{nT}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\sup_{n,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[x_{it}^{2}]\leq M_{X}.

This proves part (iv) and it completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 3
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\sqrt{n}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{n\sqrt{T}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iii)

\min(n,\sqrt{T})\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
and
\min(n,\sqrt{nT})\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iv)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|=O(1)
;

(v)

\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.
For part (i), by Assumption
4
, Lemma
1
(iii) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nT^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{jt}\xi_{it}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1\ldots,r}\max_{i=1\ldots,n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}F_{jt}\xi_{is}F_{js}]

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1\ldots,r}\max_{i=1\ldots,n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]\,\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}|\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]|\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}\leq\frac{rM_{F}M_{3\xi}}{T},

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and
s
and
M_{3\xi}
is independent of
i
. This proves part (i).

For part (ii), let
\bm{\zeta}_{i}=(\xi_{it}\cdots\xi_{iT})^{\prime}
, then by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{n\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{i}\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{i}\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=n}^{T}\lambda_{ij}\xi_{it}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{n^{2}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{ij}\lambda_{kj}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{kt}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}^{2}}{n^{2}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{kt}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{\xi}}{n},

since
M_{\Lambda}
is independent of
i
and
k
and
M_{\xi}
is independent of
t
. This proves part (ii).

For part (iii), first notice that, by Lemmas
1
(v) and
4
(ii)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n}=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(B5)

Similarly, by Lemmas
2
(i) and
1
(v)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(B6)

Then, because of Assumption
2
(c),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n^{3/2}T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\lambda_{ik}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\lambda_{ik}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}n}{n^{2}T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}K_{\xi}}{nT},

(B7)

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
j
. By using (
B7
) into (
B6
),
we prove part (iii).

Part (iv) follows from Lemma
2
(i) since

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}.

Likewise, part (v) follows from Lemma
2
(ii) since

\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\leq rM_{F}.

This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 4
.

(i)

Under either Assumption
6
, or
7
, or
8
, for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

Under Assumption
2
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.

Because of Assumption
6
, for any
i,j=1,\ldots,r
, letting
\gamma_{ij}^{F}
be the
(i,j)
th entry of
\bm{\Gamma}^{F}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\left\{\mathbb{E}[F_{it}F_{jt}F_{is}F_{js}]-\mathbb{E}[F_{it}F_{jt}]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\left\{\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}=1}^{r}C_{k_{1},ih_{1}}C_{k_{2},jh_{2}}C_{k_{3},ih_{3}}C_{k_{4},jh_{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{h_{1},t-k_{1}}u_{h_{2},t-k_{2}}u_{h_{3},s-k_{3}}u_{h_{4},s-k_{4}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\;\;\left.-\left(\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}=1}^{r}C_{k_{1},ih_{1}}C_{k_{2},jh_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{h_{1},t-k_{1}}u_{h_{2},t-k_{2}}\right]\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{C}^{4}}{T^{2}}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[u_{ht}^{4}]+\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\sum_{h_{1}=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[u_{h_{1}t}^{2}]\sum_{h_{3}=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[u_{h_{3}s}^{2}]-\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{h=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[u_{h,t}^{2}]\right)^{2}\right\}\leq\frac{M_{C}^{4}rK_{u}}{T}.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq r^{2}\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{r^{3}M_{C}^{4}K_{u}}{T},

since
M_{C}
and
K_{u}
do not depend on
i
and
j
. This proves part (i) under Assumption
6
.

Alternatively, because of Assumption
7
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{jt}F_{kt}-\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{kt}]\right\}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{T^{2}}\max_{j,k=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{F_{jt}F_{kt}-\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{kt}]\right\}\left\{F_{js}F_{ks}-\mathbb{E}[F_{js}F_{ks}]\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{T^{2}}\max_{j,k=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[F_{jt}F_{kt}F_{js}F_{ks}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[F_{jt}F_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[F_{js}F_{ks}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}}{T^{2}}\max_{j,k=1,\ldots,r}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[F_{jt}F_{kt}F_{js}F_{ks}\right]\right|+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[F_{jt}F_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[F_{js}F_{ks}\right]\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}C_{F}}{T},

since
C_{F}
is independent of
j
and
k
. This proves part (i) under Assumption
7
.

Let us now consider the case in which we make Assumption
8
.
For any
i,j=1,\ldots,r
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{r}C_{k,i\ell}C_{k^{\prime},j\ell^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{u_{\ell,t-k}u_{\ell^{\prime},t-k^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[u_{\ell,t-k}u_{\ell^{\prime},t-k^{\prime}}\right]\right\}\right).

(B8)

For simplicity let

\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{u_{\ell,t-k}u_{\ell^{\prime},t-k^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[u_{\ell,t-k}u_{\ell^{\prime},t-k^{\prime}}\right]\right\}

Then, from (
B8
), because of Assumption
8
(P1), and Minkowsky inequality

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{r}C_{k,i\ell}C_{k^{\prime},j\ell^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{r}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|C_{k,i\ell}C_{k^{\prime},j\ell^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{r}\left|C_{k,i\ell}\right|\,\left|C_{k^{\prime},j\ell^{\prime}}\right|\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{C}^{2}r^{2}\sup_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,r}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}.

(B9)

Now, since as explained in the text we can always choose
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{q}
,
then, from
Zhang and Wu 2021
, for any
\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,r
,
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
and
z>0
,
(see also
Barigozzi
et al. 2022
, Lemma C.4 and Remark C.1
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|>z\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{q,\alpha}T(\Phi_{q,\alpha}^{u})^{q}}{(Tz)^{q/2}}+C_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{Tz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}(\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{u})^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{q,\alpha}TM_{u,q}^{q}}{(Tz)^{q/2}}+C_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{Tz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}M_{u,4}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{z^{q/2}T^{q/2-1}}+C_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{Tz^{2}}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right),

(B10)

where
q>4
,
C
is a finite positive real independent of
T
,
i
,
j
,
\alpha
, and
q
, while
M_{u,q}
,
C_{q,\alpha}
,
C_{\alpha}
,
C_{1,q,\alpha}
, and
C_{2,\alpha}
are finite positive reals depending only on their subscripts. So, as expected, from (
B10
) we have that
|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(T^{-1/2},T^{2/q-1}\right)\right)=O_{\mathrm{P}}(T^{-1/2})
since
q>4
.

Moreover, from (
B10
) it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}>y\right)\mathrm{d}y\right]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}>y\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r_{T}}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}>y\right)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{r_{T}}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}>y\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r_{T}}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|>\sqrt{y}\right)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{r_{T}}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|>\sqrt{y}\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq r_{T}+\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{T^{q/2-1}}\int_{r_{T}^{2}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{y^{q/4}}\mathrm{d}y+C_{0}\int_{r_{T}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{Ty}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=r_{T}-\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{(q/4-1)T^{q/2-1}}\left.\frac{1}{y^{q/4-1}}\right|_{r_{T}}^{\infty}-\frac{C_{0}C_{2,\alpha}}{T}\left.\exp\left(-\frac{Ty}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right)\right|_{r_{T}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle=r_{T}+\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{(q/4-1)T^{q/2-1}r_{T}^{q/4-1}}+\frac{C_{0}C_{2,\alpha}}{T}\exp\left(-\frac{Tr_{T}}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right).

(B11)

Then, by choosing
r_{T}=\frac{1}{T}
from (
B11
) and since
q>4
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{C}_{kk^{\prime},\ell\ell^{\prime},T}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}+\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{(q/4-1)T^{q/2-1}T^{1-q/4}}+\frac{C_{0}C_{2,\alpha}}{T}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\left(1+C_{0}C_{2,\alpha}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right)\right)+\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{(q/4-1)T^{q/4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\left(1+C_{0}C_{2,\alpha}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{2,\alpha}}\right)+\frac{C_{1,q,\alpha}}{(q/4-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{u,\alpha,q}}{T},\;\text{say,}

(B12)

where
C_{u,\alpha,q}
is independent of
k
,
k^{\prime}
,
\ell
, and
\ell^{\prime}
.

By substituting (
B12
) into (
B9
), we get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{M_{C}^{4}r^{4}C_{u,\alpha,q}}{T}.

(B13)

Finally, from (
B13
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq r^{2}\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{it}F_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{F}\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{M_{C}^{4}r^{6}C_{u,\alpha,q}}{T},

since
M_{C}
and
C_{u,\alpha,q}
do not depend on
i
and
j
. This proves part (i) under Assumption
8
.

For part (ii), by Assumption
2
(c), letting
\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}
be the
(i,j)
the entry of
\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}\right\}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\frac{1}{T^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}\right\}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{\xi}}{T},

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
j
. This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 5
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
,
for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i-a)

\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(i-b)

if also Assumption
6
or
7
or
8
hold, then
\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\frac{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}{n}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iii)

\frac{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iv)

as
n\to\infty
,
{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, where
\mathbf{J}
is
r\times r
diagonal with entries
\pm 1
.

Proof.

For part (i-a), from Assumption
1
(c), and Lemmas
3
(i),
4
(ii), and
2
(i):

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\}\right\|+\left\|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|+\left\|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right).

For part (i-b), if we make either Assumption
6
or
7
or
8
, from Lemma
4
(i),
4
(ii) and
2
(i):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{4}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T},

where
K_{F}=r^{3}M_{C}^{4}K_{u}
under Assumption
6
, or
K_{F}=r^{2}C_{F}
under Assumption
7
, or
K_{F}=M_{C}^{4}r^{6}C_{u,\alpha,q}
under Assumption
8
. This proves part (i).

Part (ii), follows from

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n^{2}}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{M_{\xi}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right),\;\text{say,}

because of part (i) and Lemma
1
(v) and where
M_{1}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
.

For part (iii), for any
j=1,\ldots,r
, because of Weyl’s inequality and part (ii), it holds that

|\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}|\leq\mu_{1}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})=\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|.

(B14)

Hence, from (
B14
) and part (ii),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{x}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}\right)^{2}\right]\leq r\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{rM_{\xi}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq rM_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right).

(B15)

This proves part (iii).

For part (iv), because of Theorem 2 in
Yu
et al. 2015
, which is a special case of Davis Kahn Theorem, there exists an
r\times r
diagonal matrix
\mathbf{J}
with entries
\pm 1
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|}{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)},

(B16)

where
\mu_{0}^{\chi}=\infty
. This holds provided the eigenvalues
\mu_{j}^{\chi}
are distinct as required by Assumption
3
.
Therefore, from (
B16
), part (ii) and Lemma
1
(iv), and since
\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}=0
, as
n\to\infty

\displaystyle\min(n^{2},T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min(n^{2},T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}\leq\frac{8rM_{1}}{\underline{C}_{r}^{2}}=M_{2},\;\text{say,}

where
M_{2}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
.
This proves part (iv). This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 6
.

Let
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
be the
n
-dimensional vector with one in entry
i
and zero elsewhere. Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, for all
i=1,\ldots,n
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
and as
n\to\infty

(i)

\frac{\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|=O(1)
;

(iii)

{\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}^{x\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{v}^{\chi\prime}\mathbf{J}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.
First notice that

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-[\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}]_{ij}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-[\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}]_{ij}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{\xi}}{T},

(B17)

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
j
. Therefore, from Lemma
4
(i) and Lemma
2
(i), and using (
B17
),

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\,\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T},

(B18)

since
M_{\Lambda}
and
K_{F}
are independent of
i
. Then, following the same arguments as Lemma
5
(ii), because of (
B18
) and Lemma
1
(v):

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{M_{\xi}}{n}\leq M_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{T},\frac{1}{n}\right),

since
\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\|=1
and where
M_{1}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
defined in Lemma
5
(ii). This proves part (i).

For part (ii), notice that for all
i=1,\ldots,n
we must have:

\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\chi_{it})=\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\lambda}_{i}\leq\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{F},

(B19)

which is finite for all
i
and
t
. So, since by Lemma
1
(iv)

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\chi_{it})=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mu_{j}^{\chi}[\mathbf{V}^{\chi}]_{ij}^{2}\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mu_{r}^{\chi}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}[\mathbf{V}^{\chi}]_{ij}^{2}\geq\underline{C}_{r}n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2},

then, because of (
B19
), we must have, as
n\to\infty
,

\underline{C}_{r}n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{F}

which implies that, as
n\to\infty
,

n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2}\leq M_{V},

for some finite positive real
M_{V}
independent of
n
. This proves part (ii).

Finally, using the same arguments in Lemma
5
(iv), from (
B16
), part (i) and Lemma
1
(iv), and since
\mu_{0}^{\chi}=\infty
and
\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}=0
, as
n\to\infty

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\min(n,T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x\prime}-\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi\prime}\mathbf{J})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\min(n,T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\sqrt{n}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\ \frac{\min(n,T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n^{2}}n\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\ \frac{\min(n,T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8rM_{1}}{\underline{C}_{r}^{2}}=M_{2},

where
M_{2}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
defined in Lemma
5
(iv).
This proves part (iii) and completes the proof.
\Box

B.2
Minor auxiliary lemmata

Lemma 7
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n})^{-1}\|=O(1)
;

(iii)

\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(iv)

\|(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n})^{-1}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

Proof.
Parts (i) and (ii) follow directly from Lemma
1
(iv), indeed,

\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|=\frac{\mu_{1}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\overline{C}_{1},

and

\left\|\left(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\frac{n}{\mu_{r}^{\chi}}\leq\frac{1}{\underline{C}_{r}}.

Both statements hold for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
since the eigenvalues are an increasing sequence in
n
.

For part (iii), because of part (i) and Lemma
5
(iii),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}-\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|\leq\overline{C}_{1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right).

For part (iv) just notice that, because of Lemma
5
(iii) and part (ii), then
\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}
is positive definite with probability tending to one as
n,T\to\infty
.
This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 8
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
,

(i)

\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\|=o(1)
, as
n\to\infty
and
\|\bm{V}_{0}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\|=o_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, as
n,T\to\infty
;

(iii)

\|(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n})^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=o(1)
, as
n\to\infty
and
\|\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=O(1)

(iv)

\|(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n})^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=o_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

where
\bm{V}_{0}
is
r\times r
diagonal with entries the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}
sorted in descending order.

Proof.
For part (i), first notice that the
r
non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}
are also the
r
eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
which in turn are also the entries of
\bm{V}_{0}
. The proof follows from continuity of eigenvalues and since, because of Assumption
1
(a), as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}-(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\right\|=o(1).

Part (ii) is a consequence of part (i) and Lemma
7
(iii).

For part (iii) notice that

\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\right\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\right\|,

then the proof follows from part (i), Lemma
7
(ii), and since
\bm{V}_{0}
is positive definite since
\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{F}
are positive definite by Assumptions
1
(a) and
1
(b), respectively. This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 9
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, as
n\to\infty
,

(i)

\|{\mathbf{K}}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|{\mathbf{K}}^{-1}\|=O(1)
.

Proof.
For part (a), from (
A38
) in the proof of Proposition
4
(a) it follows that

\left\|\mathbf{K}-\mathbf{J}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}\right\|=o(1),

(B20)

where
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is the
r\times r
matrix of normalized eigenvectors of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
and
\mathbf{J}
is a diagonal matrix with entries
\pm 1
. Part (i) follows from the fact that
\|\mathbf{J}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}\|=O(1)
, since
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
is finite. Likewise part (ii) follows from the fact that
\mathbf{J}
is obviously positive definite and
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is also positive definite because the eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
are distinct by Assumption
3
. This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 10
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, as
n\to\infty
,

(i)

\|{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}^{-1}\|=O(1)
.

Proof.
From (
A9
) in the proof of Proposition
1
(c)
\bm{\mathcal{H}}=(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{J}
. Then, part (i) and (ii) follow immediately from Assumption
1
(b), Lemma
9
(i),
9
(ii), and since
\mathbf{J}
is obviously finite and positive definite. This completes the proof.
\Box

Lemma 11
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
4
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

(i)

\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(ii)

\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

Proof.
From (
28
), by Proposition
1
(a), Lemma
3
(v),
7
(iv)
10
(i), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right),

which proves part (i).

For part (ii), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,

(B21)

Now, because of Proposition
1
(a), Lemma
2
(i),
10
(i), and Assumption
1
(a),

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{n}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

And since by Assumption
1
(a) and Lemma
10
(ii),
\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is positive definite, then
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}
is positive definite with probability tending to one, as
n,T\to\infty
, i.e.,

\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(B22)

Moreover, because of Lemma
4
and Assumption
1
(b),
\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}
is positive definite with probability tending to one, as
T\to\infty
, i.e.,

\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(B23)

Then, because of Lemma
7
(iii), using (
B22
) and (
B23
) in (
B21
), we prove part (ii).
This completes the proof.
\Box
